- **reproai-check**: One-shot static pre-diagnose of a replication package. Scans the working directory, applies the author rule set and a venue profile, and writes 4 JSON reports (5 with a certification target). Never runs author code. Use when an author wants to know if their replication package is ready to submit, or what to fix before a reproducibility check. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/check.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-comply**: Check a replication package against a target journal's replication-package rules (AEA, ...), without re-running the full pre-diagnose. Use when the author only wants the venue compliance checklist. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/comply.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-contribute**: Contribute back to reproai — file a structured GitHub issue for a defect reproai missed, a false flag, a fix that worked, or a new journal venue and its rules. reproai never posts; it hands you a pre-filled issue URL you click Submit on. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/contribute.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-debug**: Rehearse a replication package on a COPY the way a certification service will (R and Python run twice under fixed settings and the outputs compared; Stata run once) — confirm the code runs without errors, that the Table/Figure output commands actually produced files, and whether two runs agree. On a runtime error it does NOT auto-fix; it explains the root cause, offers a few fix options, and asks the author to choose (AskUserQuestion), then applies the chosen fix and re-runs. It executes code but never compares coefficients and never issues a reproducibility verdict. Use after /reproai-fix to… ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/debug.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-fix**: Apply reproai's advisory fixes to a COPY of the package by reading the diagnostic report and rewriting the code yourself, then re-checking until the findings clear. You (the host LLM) do the rewriting under a lossless contract; the deterministic engine only decides what is wrong and re-verifies. Never touches the original; never changes what the code computes. Use after /reproai check when the author wants the recommended fixes applied. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/fix.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-map**: ADVISORY overlay of a manuscript's figures and tables on a replication package's output (write) nodes — which exhibits have no producing output, which outputs no exhibit uses, and panel/count mismatches. LaTeX-only, never runs author code, never issues a reproducibility verdict, and is deliberately NOT part of check. Use when an author wants to see whether every paper exhibit maps to a producing script and vice versa. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/map.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-prepare**: The whole preparation in one pass, from a messy replication folder to a prepared copy with every approved repair recorded, a rehearsal run on a copy (with permission), and a handoff for the destination (a journal's check, a repository, or IQSS Certify); for Certify, it then offers to lead the author to the draft dataset's Certify button. Use when an author wants their package made ready, not only diagnosed. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/prepare.md) · ★19)
- **reproai-update**: Show the installed reproai knowledge version and how to update to the latest rule set. Use when the author asks whether their rules are current, or how reproai stays up to date. ([reproai/reproai](https://github.com/reproai/reproai/blob/HEAD/claude-plugin/commands/update.md) · ★19)
